\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}L^{1,0}_{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}}\bar{\partial}f=0
then
\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}L^{1,0}_{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}}\bar{\partial}u=0
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.10

\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}L^{1,0}_{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}}\bar{\partial}u
is independent of the choice of representative
u
of
f
, so it suffices to find a representative
u
with properties a – c.

Let
u^{o}\in H^{0}(X,{\mathscr{C}}^{\infty}(\Lambda^{n,0}_{X}\otimes E))
be some initial representative of
f
. Using local coordinates
(t^{1},...,t^{m})
near
t
, we can write

\bar{\partial}u^{o}=\psi+dt^{\mu}\wedge\alpha^{o}_{\mu}+d\bar{t}^{\nu}\wedge\beta_{\bar{\nu}}\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla^{1,0}u^{o}=dt^{\mu}\wedge\phi^{o}_{\mu},

where
\alpha^{o}_{\mu}
are
E
-valued
(n-1,1)
-forms,
\beta_{\bar{\nu}}
are
E
-valued
(n,0)
-forms, and
\psi
is an
E
-valued
(n,1)
-form such that
\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\psi=0
at
t
. (See Proposition
4.23
.) The latter condition implies that
\iota^{*}_{X_{t}}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\psi)
independent of the choice of lift
\theta
. Since the horizontal lift
\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}
is linear in
\tau
, we may write

\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\psi)=:\tau^{\mu}\gamma_{\mu}

for some
E
-valued
(n-1,1)
-forms
\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{m}
on
X_{t}
.

Any other representative
u
is of the form
u=u^{o}+dt^{\mu}\wedge v_{\mu}
for some
E
-valued
(n-1,0)
-forms
v_{\mu}
, and thus

\bar{\partial}u=\psi+dt^{\mu}\wedge(\alpha^{o}_{\mu}-\bar{\partial}v_{\mu})+d\bar{t}^{\nu}\wedge\beta_{\bar{\nu}}\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla^{1,0}u=dt^{\mu}\wedge(\phi^{o}_{\mu}-\nabla^{1,0}v_{\mu}).

Therefore we seek
v_{\mu}
satisfying

\omega_{t}\wedge(\gamma_{\mu}+\iota^{*}_{X_{t}}\alpha^{o}_{\mu}-\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\bar{\partial}v_{\mu})=0\quad\text{ and }\quad\nabla^{1,0}v_{\mu}=P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\phi^{o}_{\mu}-\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}\ell).

Note that

\bar{\partial}(\omega\wedge u^{o})=\omega\wedge\bar{\partial}u^{o}=\omega\wedge\psi+dt^{\mu}\wedge\alpha^{o}_{\mu}\wedge\omega+d\bar{t}^{\nu}\wedge\beta_{\bar{\nu}}\wedge\omega,

and hence

\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\bar{\partial}(\omega\wedge u^{o})\right)=\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}((\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\omega)\wedge\psi)+\omega_{t}\wedge(\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\psi)+\tau^{\mu}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o}_{\mu})=\tau^{\mu}\omega_{t}\wedge(\gamma_{\mu}+\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o}_{\mu}).

On the other hand

\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\bar{\partial}(\omega\wedge u^{o})\right)=\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left((\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\tau})\lrcorner(\omega\wedge u^{o})-\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\omega\wedge u^{o}))\right)=-\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\omega\wedge u^{o}))\right),

where the second equality holds because
\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}
is vertical and
\omega\wedge u^{o}
, being of bidegree
(n+1,1)
, is of the form
\omega\wedge u^{o}=dt^{\mu}\wedge c_{\mu}
, and hence vanishes along fibers. Moreover
\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\omega\wedge u^{o})=\tau^{\mu}c_{\mu}+dt^{\mu}\wedge(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner c_{\mu})
, so that
\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\omega\wedge u^{o}))=\tau^{\mu}\bar{\partial}c_{\mu}+dt^{\mu}\wedge\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner c_{\mu})
. Restricting to
X_{t}
, we see that
\omega_{t}\wedge(\gamma_{\mu}+\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o}_{\mu})
is
\bar{\partial}
-exact on
X_{t}
. Therefore the
E|_{X_{t}}
-valued
(n-1,0)
-form

V^{1}_{\mu}:=\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\bar{\partial}^{*}G_{t}\left(\omega_{t}\wedge(\gamma_{\mu}+\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o}_{\mu})\right)

satisfies

\displaystyle\omega_{t}\wedge(\bar{\partial}V^{1}_{\mu}-(\gamma_{\mu}+\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o}_{\mu}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}(\omega_{t}\wedge V^{1}_{\mu})-\omega_{t}\wedge(\gamma_{\mu}+\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o}_{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}\bar{\partial}^{*}G_{t}(\omega_{t}\wedge(\gamma_{\mu}+\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o}))-\omega_{t}\wedge(\gamma_{\mu}+\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o})=0.

The second equality follows because
[L_{\omega_{t}},\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}]=L_{\omega_{t}}\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}={\rm Id}
on (
E
-valued)
(n,1)
-forms, and in the last equality we have used that
\Box=\bar{\partial}\bar{\partial}^{*}
on
\bar{\partial}
-closed forms. We note that, by the Hodge identity
\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}[\nabla^{1,0},\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}]=\bar{\partial}^{*}
,

\nabla^{1,0}V^{1}_{\mu}=\nabla^{1,0}\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\bar{\partial}^{*}G_{t}(\omega_{t}\wedge\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o}_{\mu})=[\nabla^{1,0},\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}]\bar{\partial}^{*}G_{t}(\omega_{t}\wedge\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\alpha^{o}_{\mu})=0.

Next,
P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\phi^{o}_{\mu}
is trivially orthogonal to the holomorphic
E
-valued
n
-forms, which are precisely the harmonic
E
-valued
(n,0)
-forms. By assumption, for every
\mu\in\{1,...,m\}
the twisted form
\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t^{\mu}}}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}\ell)
is orthogonal to the holomorphic
E
-valued
n
-forms. Since on
E
-valued
(n,0)
-forms
\Box=\Box^{\prime}=\nabla^{1,0}\nabla^{1,0*}
and hence
G^{\prime}=G
, the
E
-valued
(n-1,0)
-form

V^{2}_{\mu}:=G_{t}^{\prime}\nabla^{1,0*}\left(P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\phi^{o}_{\mu}-\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t^{\mu}}}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}\ell)\right)=\nabla^{1,0*}G_{t}^{\prime}\left(P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\phi^{o}_{\mu}-\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t^{\mu}}}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}\ell)\right)

solves the equation

\nabla^{1,0}V^{2}_{\mu}=P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\phi^{o}_{\mu}-\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t^{\mu}}}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}\ell)

and satisfies

\displaystyle\omega_{t}\wedge\bar{\partial}V^{2}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}L_{\omega_{t}}\nabla^{1,0*}G_{t}\left(P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\phi^{o}_{\mu}-\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t^{\mu}}}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}\ell)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}[L_{\omega_{t}},\nabla^{1,0*}]G_{t}\left(P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\phi^{o}_{\mu}-\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t^{\mu}}}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}\ell)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\bar{\partial}\bar{\partial}G_{t}\left(P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\phi^{o}_{\mu}-\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t^{\mu}}}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}\ell)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Taking
v_{\mu}
to be any smooth extension of
V_{\mu}:=V^{1}_{\mu}+V^{2}_{\mu}
to
X
, we find that the representative
u=u^{o}+dt^{\mu}\wedge v_{\mu}
satisfies Properties a and b. Finally, for
any
representative
u
of
f
one has

(32)

\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u)=(\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\tau})\lrcorner u-\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\bar{\partial}u,

so
(\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\tau})\lrcorner u
and
\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\bar{\partial}u
are
\bar{\partial}
-cohomologous (on fibers). Thus Property c holds as well.
∎

9.4.
The Proof of Theorem
3

Let us fix representatives
u_{i}
of
{\mathfrak{a}}({\mathfrak{f}}_{i})
,
i=1,2
, as in Theorem
9.2
with
\ell=0
. That is to say,
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
have the following properties.

a.

The
E|_{X_{t}}
-valued
(n-1,1)
-forms
\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\bar{\partial}u_{i})
are primitive.

b.

The
E|_{X_{t}}
-valued
(n,0)
-forms
\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}u_{i})
are holomorphic.

Moreover, by an application of Proposition
4.16
we may assume that
\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}L^{1,0}_{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}}\bar{\partial}u_{i}=0
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{I\!I}^{{\mathscr{L}}^{\theta}/{\mathscr{H}}}(\sigma){\mathfrak{f}}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}L^{1,0}_{\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}}u_{1}=P_{t}^{\perp}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\nabla^{1,0}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1})+\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\nabla^{1,0}u_{1}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{t}^{\perp}\nabla^{1,0}(\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))=\nabla^{1,0}(\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1})).

The third equality follows from Property b, while the last equality holds because the image of
\nabla^{1,0}
in
\Gamma(X,{\mathscr{C}}^{\infty}(\Lambda^{n,0}_{X}\otimes E))
is orthogonal to the kernel of
\Box^{1,0}=\Box
, and the latter kernel consists precisely of holomorphic forms. Similarly
\mathbf{I\!I}^{{\mathscr{L}}^{\theta}/{\mathscr{H}}}(\tau){\mathfrak{f}}_{2}=\nabla^{1,0}(\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}))
. Therefore

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{I\!I}^{{\mathscr{L}}^{\theta}/{\mathscr{H}}}(\sigma){\mathfrak{f}}_{1},\mathbf{I\!I}^{{\mathscr{L}}^{\theta}/{\mathscr{H}}}(\tau){\mathfrak{f}}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{t}}\left<\nabla^{1,0}(\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\nabla^{1,0}(\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}))},h\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\text{\rm-1})^{n+1}\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{t}}\left<\bar{\partial}\nabla^{1,0}(\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}-2n+1}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t})\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>

\displaystyle\qquad-(\text{\rm-1})^{n+1}\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{t}}\left<\nabla^{1,0}\bar{\partial}(\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{(n-1)^{2}}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t})(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>

\displaystyle\qquad-\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>.

Using (
22
) we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t})\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[L_{\omega_{t}},\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}]\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t})\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1})=L_{\omega_{t}}\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t})\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-L_{\omega_{t}}[\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t}),\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}]\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}),

and thus

\displaystyle\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{(n-1)^{2}}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t})(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>=-\left(\left[\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t}),\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\right]\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1},\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right).

From the differential identity
\bar{\partial}(\xi\lrcorner u)=(\bar{\partial}\xi)\lrcorner u-\xi\lrcorner\bar{\partial}u
and the algebraic identity

(a-b,a-b)=(a,a)-(b,b)-(a-b,b)-(b,a-b)

for a bilinear form
(\cdot,\cdot)
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{X_{t}}\left<((\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma})\lrcorner u_{1})\wedge\overline{((\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\tau})\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>-\int_{X_{t}}\left<\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{2}))},h\right>

\displaystyle-\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{2}))},h\right>-\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>.

Now,

\displaystyle\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>=(-1)^{n-1}\int_{X_{t}}\left<\nabla^{1,0}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}},h\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{n}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\nabla^{1,0}\bar{\partial}u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}},h\right>-(-1)^{n}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(L^{1,0}_{\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}}\bar{\partial}u_{1})\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}},h\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{n}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\nabla^{1,0}\bar{\partial}u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}},h\right>.

We claim that
\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\nabla^{1,0}\bar{\partial}u_{1}))=\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\Theta(h)u_{1}))
. Indeed, since
\nabla^{1,0}u_{1}=dt^{\mu}\wedge\phi_{\mu}
with
\phi_{\mu}
holomorphic on
X_{t}
,

\displaystyle\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}\nabla^{1,0}u_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma})\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}u_{1}-\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\nabla^{1,0}u_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle dt^{\mu}\wedge((\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma})\lrcorner\phi_{\mu})-\bar{\partial}(\sigma^{\mu}\phi_{\mu}-dt^{\mu}\wedge(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\phi_{\mu}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\partial}\phi_{\mu}+dt^{\mu}\wedge\left((\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma})\lrcorner\phi_{\mu}-\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\phi_{\mu})\right).

Both of these terms vanish on
X_{t}
, which proves our claim. Therefore

\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>=(-1)^{n}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\Theta(h)u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}},h\right>.

By interchanging
\sigma\leftrightarrow\tau
and
u_{1}\leftrightarrow u_{2}
and then taking complex conjugates,

\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{2}))},h\right>=(-1)^{n}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1})\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\Theta(h)u_{2})},h\right>

Using (
22
) again, we have

\displaystyle(-1)^{n}\int_{X_{t}}\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\left<\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\Theta(h)u_{1})\right)\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}},h\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}-2n+1}\int_{X_{t}}\left<\omega_{t}\wedge\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h)u_{1})\right)\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}},h\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h)u_{1})),\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h)\right)u_{1},\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right)-\left(\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t})(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}),\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h)\right)u_{1},\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right)+\left(\left[\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t}),\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\right](\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}),\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right),

Since
[\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t}),\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}]=\Box-\Box^{1,0}
is Hermitian symmetric for the
L^{2}
-norm, we find that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>+\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\int_{X_{t}}\left<(\bar{\partial}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{2}))},h\right>

\displaystyle\qquad=2\left(\left[\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t}),\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\right](\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}),\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad-\left(\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h)\right)u_{1},\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right)-\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1},\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h)\right)u_{2}\right).

Adding everything up yields

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{I\!I}^{{\mathscr{L}}^{\theta}/{\mathscr{H}}}(\sigma){\mathfrak{f}}_{1},\mathbf{I\!I}^{{\mathscr{L}}^{\theta}/{\mathscr{H}}}(\tau){\mathfrak{f}}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{X_{t}}\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\left<\left((\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma})\lrcorner u_{1}\right)\wedge\overline{\left((\bar{\partial}\xi^{\theta}_{\tau})\lrcorner u_{2}\right)},h\right>-\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{1}),\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\bar{\partial}u_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+([\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t}),\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}](\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}),\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\qquad-([\Lambda_{\omega_{t}},\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h))]u_{1},\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})-(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1},[\Lambda_{\omega_{t}},\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}((\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h))]u_{2}).

Thus Theorem
3
is proved.
∎

9.5.
The proof of Theorem
1

We begin with the following computation.

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
{\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2}\in\Gamma(B,{\mathscr{C}}^{\infty}({\mathscr{L}}))
be represented by
E
-valued
(n,0)
-forms
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
respectively. Then the differential
(1,1)
-form
\alpha_{h}({\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2})
on
B
defined by

\alpha_{h}({\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2}):=p_{*}\left(\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\left<(\Theta(h)u_{1})\wedge\bar{u}_{2},h\right>\right)

is given by the formula

\displaystyle\left<\alpha_{h}({\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2}),\sigma\wedge\bar{\tau}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\Theta(h)_{\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\bar{\xi}^{\theta}_{\tau}}f_{1},f_{2}\right)-\left(\left[\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h_{t}),\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\right](\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}),(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h)\right)f_{1},\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right)+\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1},\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h)\right)f_{2}\right)

for any choice of horizontal distribution
\theta
.

Proof.

Using a partition of unity and Fubini’s Theorem, one sees that

\left<\alpha_{h}({\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2}),\sigma\wedge\bar{\tau}\right>=\int_{X_{t}}\left<\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{2}\bar{\xi}^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner((\Theta(h)u_{1})\wedge\bar{u}_{2})\right),h\right>

for any horizontal distribution
\theta
. We compute that

\displaystyle\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner((\Theta(h)u_{1})\wedge\bar{u}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{1}\wedge\bar{u}_{2})+((\Theta(h)(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\bar{u}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{1}\wedge\bar{u}_{2})-((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\Theta(h)u_{2}}),

and then

\displaystyle\bar{\xi}^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner((\Theta(h)u_{1})\wedge\bar{u}_{2}))

\displaystyle=((\Theta(h)_{\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\bar{\xi}^{\theta}_{\tau}}u_{1})\wedge\bar{u}_{2})+(-1)^{n-1}((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{1}\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}})

\displaystyle+(-1)^{n}((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{2}})+(-1)^{n}((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{\Theta(h)(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})})

\displaystyle=((\Theta(h)_{\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\bar{\xi}^{\theta}_{\tau}}u_{1})\wedge\bar{u}_{2})+(-1)^{n-1}((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{1}\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}})

\displaystyle+(-1)^{n}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1})\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{2}})+(-1)^{n-1}(\Theta(h)(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})}.

But by (
22
)

\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{1})=[L_{\omega_{t}},\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}]\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{1})=L_{\omega_{t}}\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\iota_{X_{t}}^{*}((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{1}),

so

\displaystyle\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\left<(-1)^{n-1}((\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{1}\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}}),h\right>

\displaystyle=\left<\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{(n-1)^{2}}(\omega_{t}\wedge\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h))u_{1}\wedge\overline{\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}}),h\right>=\left\{\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h))u_{1},\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right\}\frac{\omega_{t}^{n}}{n!},

where
\{\cdot,\cdot\}
is the pointwise inner product, on
X_{t}
, of
E
-valued
(n-1,0)
-forms induced by
h_{t}
and
\omega_{t}
.
Similarly

(-1)^{n}\left<\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1})\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\Theta(h))u_{2}}),h\right>=\left\{\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1},\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h))u_{2}\right\}\frac{\omega_{t}^{n}}{n!}

and

\displaystyle(-1)^{n-1}\left<\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}(\Theta(h)(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}))\wedge\overline{(\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2})},h\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h)(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}),\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right\}\frac{\omega^{n}}{n!}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\{\left[\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}\Theta(h),\Lambda_{\omega_{t}}\right](\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner u_{1}),\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner u_{2}\right\}\frac{\omega^{n}}{n!}.

Integration over
X_{t}
completes the proof.
∎

Conclusion of the proof of Theorem
1
.

Fix a smooth horizontal distribution
\theta
and a point
t\in B
. Let
{\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2}\in\Gamma(B,{\mathscr{C}}^{\infty}({\mathscr{L}}))
be smooth sections such that
{\mathfrak{f}}_{i}(t)\in{\mathscr{H}}_{t}
,
i=1,2
, and take representative
E
-valued
(n,0)
-forms
u_{1},u_{2}
of
{\mathfrak{f}}_{1},{\mathfrak{f}}_{2}
as in Theorem
3
. By Theorem
2
, Theorem
3
and Proposition
9.3
we obtain

(\Theta^{{\mathscr{H}}}_{\sigma\bar{\tau}}{\mathfrak{f}}_{1}(t),{\mathfrak{f}}_{2}(t))=\left(\xi^{\theta}_{\sigma}\lrcorner\bar{\partial}u_{1},\xi^{\theta}_{\tau}\lrcorner\bar{\partial}u_{2}\right)+\left<p_{*}\left(\sqrt{\text{\rm-1}}^{n^{2}}\left<(\Theta(h)u_{1})\wedge\bar{u}_{2},h\right>\right),\sigma\wedge\bar{\tau}\right>.

The first term on the right hand side of this formula defines a Nakano-non-negative form on
{\mathscr{H}}_{t}
, and the second defines a
k
-positive (respectively
k
-non-negative) form if and only if
h
has
k
-positive (respectively
k
-non-negative) curvature. The proof is complete.
∎
